Resolution Supporting Troy Public 
WHEREAS,  the  Troy  Board  of  Education  believes  that  a  strong  public 
library is a valuable asset to the community, and 
WHEREAS,  students and employees  in the Troy School District derive 
substantial benefits from the facilities, resources and staff at the Troy 
Public Library, and 
reading, and enriches learning opportunities, and 
WHEREAS,  any  disruption  in  operation  of  the  library  would  be  a 
significant loss to our students, staff and the greater community, 
BE  IT  THEREFORE  RESOLVED,  that  the  Troy  Board  of  Education  urges 
public support for the August 2, 2011, millage vote to keep the library 
open.  Should  the  millage  fail  to  pass,  the  Troy  Board  of  Education 
further  resolves  to  urge  the  Troy  City  Council  to  take  whatever  steps 
necessary to keep the library open.  
Ayes:  7 
Nays:  0

CURRICULUM 
under the direction of this Board of Education/Governing Body of 
the Troy School District, City of Troy, County of Oakland, of the state 
of Michigan, are hereby: 
(A)  enrolled as members of the Michigan High School Athletic 
Association, Inc., a nonprofit association, and 
(B)  are further enrolled to participate in the approved 
              interschool athletic  activities sponsored by said  
              association. 
The  Board  of  Education/Governing  Body  hereby  delegates  to  the 
Superintendent  or  his/her  designee(s)  the  responsibility  for  the 
supervision  and  control  of  said  activities  and  hereby  accepts  the 
Constitution and By‐laws of said association and adopts as its own the 
rules,  regulations  and  interpretations  (as  minimum  standards)  as 
published  in  the  current  HANDBOOK  and  qualifications  as  published 
in the BULLETIN as the governing code under which the said schools

                      Contract Extension                      
On June 15, 2004, the Board of Education approved a contract for diesel fuel for the 
2004/05 school year.  In July 2011, we contacted RKA Petroleum Company to extend the 
contract through June 30, 2012 with the same terms.  RKA Petroleum has agreed to 
again extend the terms of this contract.  
Note that this contract is based on the lowest profit margin per gallon (fixed for contract 
period).  Please note that our price lock continues to be lower than that obtained by the 
Macomb/Oakland Fuel Consortium.  Our cost per gallon per delivery/profit i s $0.0045 for 
regular diesel vs. $0.0165 for the Macomb/Oakland Consortium.  Estimated annual usage 
of 160,000 gallons.  Actual contract will change based on market conditions and fuel 
usage. 
In follow-up with RKA Petroleum, I was told that our pricing is  grandfathered under the 
old contract.  Considering that we are being offered a lower price than the Consortium , 
it appears to be in our best interest to continue this contract.  
Accordingly, I recommend continuation of this bid as follows:  
 RKA Petroleum Company Initial Estimate $385,000 
Note our total cost for diesel fuel in 2010 -11 was $385,649.90 
Please include this as an action item on the August 16, 20101Board agenda as an 
expenditure of the 2011-12 General Fund.

RE:  2011-2012 Bulk Fuel Deliveries 
SENT VIA EMAIL TO flams2@troy.k12.mi.us 
Frank: 
Thank you for allowing RKA Petroleum Companies to be your fuel vendor of choice over the 
past years.   
RKA has supplied fuel products to the Troy School District for several years, and we wish to 
continue to do so.  With that in mind, RKA wishes to extend the current fuel contract another 
year, which would take us up to July 31, 2012. 
I would also like to point out that all of the RKA Petroleum Companies are certified minority 
businesses (WBE), which I can supply you with a certificate if necessary.  We would also like 
to discuss the alternative fuel, Biodiesel, with your maintenance facility in the near future.  
RKA Petroleum is a certified BQ9000 Biodiesel Distributer, which is the highest certification 
for quality control through the National Biodiesel Board.  I have enclosed some information 
on this superior product, and we hope that you will consider running this in your fleet this 
school year. 
Thank you, we look forward to doing business with you over the next year. 
Sincerely, 
Keith L. Albertie

7. CURRICULUM 
A. CONSIDERATION:  Schools of Choice - Section 105c  
             10 th Grade Only for 2011 - 2012  
In accordance with Mi chigan state law, school dist ricts shall pass a resolution 
each school year if they intend to partic ipate in a Schools of Choice program.  It 
is anticipated that seats will be ava ilable for the 2011-2012 school year for 
additional grade ten students at the In ternational Academy East.  It would be 
appropriate at this time to consider adopting a Schools of Choice section 105c 
resolution for ten non-resident 10 th grade students who attend a district in 
another county, if that Intermediate School District is contiguous with the 
Oakland Intermediate School District boundaries.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution.

 Supported by: __________________________

Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a 
limited Schools of Choice program for the 2011-2012 school year for ten non-
that Intermediate School District is co ntiguous with the Oakland Intermediate 
School District boundaries for the pu rposes of attending the International 
Academy East program located in the Troy School District.

152
Region 8 – three year term 
Donald 	Hubler	 L ’Anse 	Creuse 	Public 	Schools	 Macomb 	County**
Time served on this board:  Five years
Office held:  Treasurer
MASB Certification:
    Certified Boardmember (CBA) 
    Award of Merit 
    Award of Distinction 
      Master Boardmember 
      Master Diamond 
Election Statement: 
I am currently serving as Treasurer of the L ’Anse Creuse Public School Board and representative for Region 8 on 
the MASB Board of Directors. I have earned the MASB Level 5, Master Diamond Award certification and for the 
last two years have served as Chair of the Macomb County School Board’s Legislative Committee. In my career 
I served the City of St Clair Shores as Director of Public Works and Water. My responsibilities included budget 
preparation, contract negotiations, planning and implementation of public service programs that required com-
pliance with local, state and federal regulations. 
In my years of public service I obtained training and experience in Interest Based Bargaining, Resolution Media-
tion, and participated in Michigan Employment Commission case preparation and testimony. My work responsi-
bility was to answer “how,” and I have great appreciation for the difference between a board’s responsibilities to 
identify the “what.” The Michigan Association of School Boards have a well-organized, highly professional staff 
that has implemented a strategic plan that identifies and tracks the “how,” in response to the Board of Direc-
tor’s “what.” It is important that the Board of Directors provide clear leadership so our staff can best pursue our 
association’s goals.
Every child independent of race, sex, religion, sexual orientation, economic situation, physical or developmen-
tal challenges, has the right to a public education that is designed to meet their individual needs. It is a board 
member’s responsibility to come prepared to every meeting with an open mind and actively participate in delib-
erations by comment, discussion and to support the Board’s decisions.
Politics should be eliminated from determining funding and program requirements. We must research and sup-
port structural changes and local solutions to educational challenges. The legislative, judicial and executive 
branches of government fail to realize that it is their adversarial political positions that create a dysfunctional 
atmosphere for education. The impact of political elections that change educational goals and resources every 
2-4 or 6 years is detrimental to the environment of education.
School boards must not, try to recreate what we were, or continue to be distracted in the defense of what we 
are. MASB must focus on how to prepare our school boards to meet the challenges of a free enterprise system 
in an era that requires development of conceptual skills.

Region 8 – three year term (continued)
Robert W. McLachlan Romulus Community Schools Wayne County
Time served on this board: Two years
Office held:  Treasurer
Time served on another board: Eight years, Westwood Community School District
Offices held: Vice President, Secretary and Treasurer
MASB Certification:
      CBA Certification 
      Award of Merit 
      Award of Distinction 
      Master Boardmember
Election Statement:
I would like to serve on the MASB Board of Directors to continue to offer service to my colleagues and to “give 
back” to the organization that has taught me so many important skills to fulfill my responsibilities as a school 
board member. I am a member of the MASB Government Relations Committee, where I enjoy the opportunity 
to keep abreast of legislative trends facing our schools. I support the voluntary consolidation of school districts 
and/or services, wherein the local officials, who know what works for their communities, are making the deci-
sions that will ultimately affect the delivery of their programs to their children.  
I support tenure reform, so long as it maintains an employee’s rights to due process. I believe that there needs 
to be a paradigm shift from “funding education” to “investing in education.” Currently, it appears that the 
State of Michigan looks upon providing appropriate (I dislike the use of the word “adequate” in this context) 
funding for education as a bothersome chore rather than the opportunity, yes, responsibility, to put the re-
sources in place so that our children receive quality education and vocational training. We must put a greater 
emphasis on early childhood education programs.  
We must challenge ourselves to diversify our programs to become more outcome-based instead of relying upon 
rote scoring on standardized testing. We must expand the use of technology for our children, to afford them 
the widest spectrum of academic and enrichment programming while keeping costs controlled. I believe “seat 
time” is not the only yardstick to measure validity of a curriculum. Skill mastery must be the order of the day, 
not the traditional social passing we see in so many of our schools.
In addition to my service on the MASB Government Relations Committee, I have served on my boards’ Finance 
and Policy Committees, as well as on the Healthy Living Committee. Within the community, I have served on 
the City of Romulus Financial Task Force, and I serve as Vice-President of the Romulus Democratic Club. I am 
a former leader and sponsor of a troop in the Boy Scouts of America program, and an active member in the 
Romulus Band Boosters organization, which raised over $25,000 toward new uniforms until my guidance. I 
am an individual that is dedicated, enthusiastic, and I pursue success in everything I do. I look forward to the 
opportunity to join the Board of Directors.

Darlene L. Pomponio  Southgate Community Schools  Wayne County
Time served on this board: One year
MASB Certification:

    Award of Merit 
Election Statement:
Hello Board Members,
It is a pleasure to meet you. I may have met some of you already at the MASB conferences. I am a mother of three 
children ages 21, 19, and six. T o introduce myself I have outlined my background for your convenience. My work 
history and academic experiences together make me a strong educational leader with direction appropriate for the 
current changes that are taking place in Michigan.
•	 Vice-Chairperson – MASB Resolution and Bylaw Committee
•	 Executive Board Member – WCASB
•	 Bylaw Committee Member – WCASB
•	 Degrees held – Ph.D., Applied Management and Decision Sciences – Leadership and Organizational 
Change; MBA, Business Administration – Strategic Management; Bachelor of Science – Criminal Justice – 
Business/Criminal Law
•	 Certification – Project Management 
•	 Work history as a Corporate Manager, College Professor and Non-Profit Foundation for Education and Re -
search
•	 Writer – Newsherald, Author – Published Dissertation and articles with the Newsherald, an online newspa -
per. I am a peer reviewer for several academic and professional business journals.
•	 Other Education related experience: T utor, Writing Coach, and Course/Curriculum Development
We are in the midst of historic moments in Michigan as the importance and relevance of public education hangs 
in the balance. It is paramount that we work together as leaders to make important decisions as well as be active 
proponents for quality education for our students. Therefore, I am asking for your district’s vote in the upcoming 
nomination process. 
Please nominate me, Darlene Pomponio when your district receives the MASB Board of Director Ballot on July 22, 
2011. I will be diligent in my work to support accountability and competitive education on behalf of your students.
